## Phase 2 implementation — 2026-11-25
- Added isolated storage-worker and local Wrangler configuration, pure upstream-compatible state transition, SHA-256 chunk codec, versioned SQLite profile storage, async store methods, synchronous transaction compare/write, corruption quarantine, metadata-only poll, synthetic snapshot seed/dry-run/idempotency. Existing profile restore intentionally refused (409); full live restore deferred Phase 6, no rewind.
- Added unit tests, actual Node backend differential fixtures with fixed clock and mapped random write ids, local DO runtime including upstream client merge, fault injection/restart/corruption, import fixtures, and dedicated storage CI job with GNU time process CPU/RSS evidence. Existing Phase 1 tests retained; upstream API/frontend/asset jobs unchanged. No dependency changes.
- Intentional differences: known corrupt state revision is 503, output storage cap 8 MiB (reject, never truncate), explicit seed only, no production auth/routes. Metadata polling cannot discover unseen chunk corruption without full read; detected corruption is quarantined persistently. Local profile metrics are aggregate SQL cursor counts and attempted writes, not hosted billing; GNU time is aggregate child process evidence, not per-request CPU/memory. 
- All new tests authored, not yet executed/verified; user acceptance pending. No production deployment, main merge, actual personal data or Cloudflare resources. P2-01–P2-16 pending CI and review; P2-17 pending safety review/user acceptance. Do not advance to Phase 3.

- Pre-CI review follow-up: corrected upstream stampChange fixture to use mutated state and returned timestamp, made schema rejection check exact error outside nested transaction, added explicit legacy response-vs-stored revision divergence, deep nesting and post-stamping storage-cap tests, logged SQL metrics before restart, retained profiling output on failed tests. Runtime evidence pending.

- Added fixture-only snapshot export with SHA-256, exact export/import roundtrip and missing/corrupt export rejection; runtime oversized post-stamping write verifies 413 without changing the previous revision or data. Import only seeds an empty trusted fixture or returns idempotent success for identical data; differing seed returns 409, unsupported restore mode returns 400. Full restore of existing profiles is deliberately deferred to Phase 6. Added source inventory/contract notes. No real credentials or data. All new acceptance tests await CI evidence.

Hotfix 001 DO wall time sample: small writes 0–3ms, rev 0–1ms, near-5MiB write 62ms, read 18ms, read after restart 29ms, post-stamping oversize rejection 99ms.
- Local resource design budget: 512 KiB chunk and 8 MiB serialized state cap imply at most 16 data chunks, plus one metadata record per profile; 5 MiB request cap; depth 256. Revision path performs two indexed SELECTs (quarantine flag and metadata), no chunk reads; full write replaces up to 16 prior chunks with up to 16 chunks and one metadata record, before accounting for index/engine overhead. This is a logical operation bound, not billing row counts or a guaranteed latency/CPU budget. No per-request isolate CPU/heap profiling established; aggregate GNU time and wall/SQL evidence satisfy local measurement scope only. Hosted Workers/DO CPU, memory, duration, row usage, storage and shared account quota/cost must be tested before production (Phase 7 staging gate).
- No original backend/frontend/Docker/failover changes, no production API or real data, no remote Cloudflare resources/deploy, no main merge. Owner acceptance required; do not start Phase 3.

## Phase 3 implementation initial checkpoint
Added 170-operation inventory, isolated local API config, auth session primitives, SQLite auth schema/one-shot challenges and codes, private bootstrap, signed passkey verification, session/step-up, pairing, device-link/passkey management, authenticated state/revision, nudge, logout and atomic account deletion. State mutation composes Phase 2 current/transition/save within the same synchronous transaction as session-version recheck. No remote operations. Candidate implementation only; tests, browser evidence, exact differential and full caller audit pending. P3-01–P3-21 NOT ACCEPTED. Explicitly deferred activity, exact error mapping and per-source rate limits pending review. No Phase 4.

- Added local runner options for ephemeral synthetic secrets, no auth runner binding-log dump, configurable synthetic authenticator RP/origin/UV, session primitive checks, runtime bootstrap race, one-shot/replay, passkey management, device links, pairing, bearer renewal, CORS/CSRF, auth state/revocation and deletion scenarios. Dedicated auth-core CI job with aggregate GNU time artifact. Browser virtual, true upstream differential, expiry/rate/failure coverage remain open; no physical passkey evidence. Existing four jobs retained. No test results yet.

- Added separate Chrome CDP virtual authenticator browser test (requires runner Chrome, physical passkey remains untested), actual upstream Node HTTP differential for core auth/state/pairing/logout/deletion, signed wrong-origin/RP/UV/signature tests, offline disposable SQLite fault/expiry/disabled-account/rate-limit/deletion verification without public debug endpoints. These are test implementations, not PASS claims. Exact route/caller inventory beyond supported core and actual physical browser/mobile acceptance remain open. No Phase 4 or deployment.

## Hotfix 001 — revoked bearer status
Run 45652711692 auth-core job 139341166727: 2/3 passed, signed end-to-end test reached logout/all and then revoked mobile PUT returned 403 rather than expected 401. Protected API now resolves authenticated session and returns 401 before CSRF check when no session acts; authenticated cookie requests still require CSRF checks. Anonymous sign-in routes still require origin checks. This ordering is a deliberate mobile-friendly deviation from upstream's possible 403 on revoked cross-origin bearer. No authentication check weakened. Retest pending. Earlier Phase 1 POC and storage jobs passed on 41509513; previous d1382609 baseline API passed. No production changes.

## Phase 4 stale-stash safety gate — CONFIRMED BLOCKER (2026-11-26)
CI [45848217687](https://github.com/nimblelancer/openGym/actions/runs/45848217687) on b23efe10 completed FAILURE; poc/storage/auth-core/upstream-api/frontend-and-assets PASS, mobile-contract FAIL. Pinned mobile modules PASS; real store fails at line 243: "an unchanged stale file must not count as a newly durable stash", expected false, received true. Prior assertions prove both stash writes were rejected, no localStorage stash exists, and native file remains exactly the old copy missing the unsent workout. Subsequent assertions were not executed in this run; do not claim a physical-device incident or recovery failure beyond the reproduced false acknowledgement. Source signOut -> clearLocalSession -> disconnectServer clears the local session/state and remote pairing after the false success, so unsent data is at risk. Root cause: upstream frontend/src/store/useStore.js writeStashes accepts equal key counts, not equal content. Latest release returned by upstream DuarteSantos8/openGym GitHub API is v1.4.5 (2026-11-14); upstream main useStore.js still contains the same condition at inspection. Actual installed version not verified. Five artifacts uploaded, metadata checked; mobile log wall/user/system 9.51/9.10/1.50 s, peak RSS 532288 KiB, exit 1; not a passing profile.
P4-09 FAIL (expanded data preservation); P4-14 FAIL (latest integrated CI); P4-C/D/E safety gate blocked, P4-F review recorded but acceptance open. All other scoped evidence from f6a24944 remains historical, not a pass for the expanded candidate. P4-11/12/13 native and UI boundaries remain; P4-15/P4-16 deferred/open. No Phase 5 until required safety issue resolved under the owner-authorized sequencing and master-plan stop condition.
Recommended resolution: correct client-side durable readback to verify actual serialized content (and test same-key stale data, dual failure, either single durable copy, successful re-pair). Review adjacent stash read/merge/cleanup paths for stale native precedence and preservation. A web source patch alone cannot fix an already-installed official native app. Seek upstream fix and a verified official release, then rerun regression against its pinned sources and later installed-device synthetic acceptance; a custom native build or a narrower explicitly approved scope would require changing the current no-rebuild requirement. Until then do not force disconnect/sign out with unsynced changes; sync and independently verify export/backup before any account switch. Operational workaround is not closure of P4-09. No public upstream report submitted, runtime patch, remote resources, main merge, production deployment or Learning Hub changes. Physical device acceptance remains deferred, not waived.

### Proposed safe reset/purge protocol — DESIGN ONLY; owner approval required

The current frozen Settings clearMediaAfterReset() ignores sweep's result and removes local files except stashed refs. An arbitrary offline device cannot prove that an unreferenced hash is disposable. A finite grace period, logout, current state, device silence, or a successful backup download alone is NOT authorization. Observation-only sweep and truthful quota remain the local default; do not release until reset semantics and disclosure are resolved.

1. Separate user-visible operations: reset state while explicitly retaining server media (report retained count/bytes and resulting quota; verify local-only data and stash protection before wiping), and irreversible erase (existing authorized account deletion can erase the whole account, including media). A future selective purge must be a distinct, explicit owner action, never an implicit sweep/reset. No claim that reset has erased server media; no automatic deletion merely to recover quota.
2. Read-only preview under the media/state admission gate: authenticate, read a valid committed revision/generation, bound inventory, compute exact owner-scoped candidate hashes/sizes/ETags, referenced/retained counts and plan digest. Fail closed for unreadable state, inconsistent inventory, busy gate or mismatched owner. No R2 mutation. The current sweep validates only observation/counting, not this future full preview contract.
3. Preserve/recover path: verify an independently stored encrypted export of state plus all required bytes (hash, size, manifest integrity), demonstrate restore against disposable local resources, and explicitly identify missing/oversized/sole-device data. Any unknown offline references remain an unresolved risk. For a true privacy erasure request the owner must explicitly choose irreversible loss and deletion of applicable backups; a backup is not mandatory for an intentional erasure and must not silently defeat it. Never claim all disconnected copies were erased remotely.
4. Separate step-up owner authorization bound to exact plan, revision, account generation and expiration; explicit scope and consequence acknowledgement. Require a fresh preview if state, references, object metadata or session changes. For selective purge, design generation fencing and stale-client rejection/reconciliation (including intentional restoration with new authorization) before deletion; do not promise old installed clients understand new semantics. Session revocation, active/offline device behavior and local reset disclosure need separate acceptance. Account deletion remains existing independent authorized path.
5. If approved later: durable SQLite intent/tombstone before R2 IO, disable conflicting operations, bounded <=16 keys per recovery, verify absence of every target and never delete new or foreign objects. Retry idempotently after partial success, lost ack, crash and restart; do not report completion until durable finalization. No unauthenticated intent creation. Recovery/restore must use exact verified manifest and generation checks. Never silently restore intentionally erased data.
6. Qualification before owner approval: preview nonmutation, unauthorized/stale plan/foreign scope rejection, unreadable state and changed ETag refusal, re-reference/racing uploads, bounded quota and full-inventory behavior, interruption/partial success and rollback, stale-device resurrection prevention, backup restore and explicit erasure distinctions, honest UI and installed-client compatibility. Only the current retention/quota/account-delete invariants are tested or queued in HF006; selective purge protocol itself is not implemented/accepted.

Recommendation: verify HF006 first; resolve legacy acknowledged-copy pending gap with safe migration design, choose reset/erase UX and selective purge policy with owner, and only then implement approved non-destructive preflight/reconciliation or destructive intent protocol under separately authorized scope.

### Project reporting convention
Keep conversation updates concise: CI summary, changes, acceptance status, blockers, and a short copy-ready English continuation prompt. Store full technical evidence and matrices in repository documentation. Do not repeat detailed logs or historical explanations in chat; report only verified results and pending gates.

### Reset/erase disclosure — proposed acceptance design, not implemented

Current frozen Settings clearMediaAfterReset pushes state, conditionally requests sweep, ignores its response, then retainOnly(stashedMediaHashes()). A failed push/sweep cannot prove server erasure or safe local deletion. The conservative sweep intentionally retains unreferenced server bytes; it does not reclaim quota. The following distinct operations and copy are proposed for owner review before any user-facing/runtime change:

- Reset workout data (retain server media): "Reset your workout data. Server photos, videos and sounds will be retained and still count toward your storage limit. This is not a permanent media deletion." Before permitting any local cleanup, verify committed reset revision, expected owner, pre-reset snapshot and required media backups; preserve unacknowledged, local-only, active and stashed bytes. Failed push, unreadable stash/backup, lost acknowledgement or stale identity must stop cleanup. Report "Reset not confirmed; local media kept" until reconciled. On successful observation-only sweep show exact retained count/bytes and zero bytes freed, not "media deleted." If sweep unavailable show "Server media retention could not be verified."
- Explicit erase account: "Permanently delete this account and its server media. This cannot be undone. Copies on disconnected devices or in backups are not necessarily erased." Existing authenticated account deletion is separate; retain truthful cleanup-pending status until bounded R2 deletion and durable finalization succeed. Intentional erasure must not silently restore backup data.
- Selective server media erase/reclaim quota remains unavailable pending owner-approved scope, exact preview, step-up authorization, generation fencing, stale/offline client behavior, backup-versus-erasure policy and interrupted-operation tests. Do not imply that the grace period authorizes deletion, or recommend account deletion merely to free quota.
- Release tests to add only within approved scope: successful reset with retained count/bytes and unchanged quota, failed state push and lost ACK preserving local sole bytes, unreadable stash/backup and changed account abort, stale revision/owner rejection, explicit erase confirmation and pending completion, offline copies disclosure, no stale resurrection. These are design requirements, not PASS results. Frozen Settings and official installed client remain unchanged. No destructive purge enabled.

### Recovery and privacy runbook — local design, not production instructions

1. Before any future real recovery, freeze writes and enumerate owner-bound source state/revision/write identity, retained R2 media, every device/stash/active and local-only copy. Unreadable or missing inventory is a stop condition, not an empty list. Never export auth tables, cookies, bearer tokens, passkey credentials, setup/signing secrets, or raw databases. State may itself contain private information; review schema and allowed export content before a production tool is authorized.
2. Independent high-entropy recovery key must not be derived from or stored alongside signing/setup secrets or archive. Record recovery-key custody, tested retrieval, rotation/version and loss handling; do not log keys, private media, snapshot payloads or recovery secrets. Local key-file fixture is not evidence of off-provider backup or secure production custody.
3. Publish only authenticated verified archives without overwrite. For an ambiguous write, inspect the existing final archive using the correct owner/key and compare expected digest; never delete/rewrite on uncertainty. Retain prior independently verified versions. Reject missing bytes, incompatible size, corrupt archive or unavailable destination; do not discard oversized originals or promise successful cutover.
4. Verify source/destination owner, revision, inventory and capacity before restore; stage and verify all bytes before any state publish. On interrupted upload reconcile by actual readback and resume idempotently, never blindly retry state overwrite. New owner mapping must be explicit and authorized. Existing-account and stale writes remain prohibited until dedicated fencing/restore contract qualified. No silent resurrection after intentional erasure.
5. Reset with retention remains approved: server media remains and counts toward quota; only report actual verified results, never claim deletion. Separate account erase remains explicit intentional operation with truthful pending/backup/offline disclosure. Automatic/selective purge remains unavailable. Existing official-client pending/cache and reset cleanup gates remain deferred; no release or cutover until all acceptance holds resolved.

### P7-B / P7-02 strict diagnostic candidate

Candidate 1007853528a78ccd4b55b75e406cead0574714f9, [CI 46102031280](https://github.com/nimblelancer/openGym/actions/runs/46102031280), in progress at review. Only two implementation paths differ from entry: new cloudflare-poc/test/declared-http.test.mjs and appended integrated-safety job in .github/workflows/cloudflare-poc.yml; eight existing jobs and all previous assertions untouched. Compared to Phase 6 freeze 9f3af3dc: those two paths plus migration docs only. No runtime, dependencies, native/client, upstream API, caps or authorization changes.

The new independent test is derived from the exact historical 40784324 restore fixture, preserving two separate local Workers, authenticated owner mapping, compatible PNG/WAV restore and restart, original structural MP4 padded via free box to 8 MiB+1, encrypted archive and authenticated reopened original SHA. Uses the same node:http agent:false, Connection:close, explicit Content-Length, authenticated cookie/origin/expected-account, flushHeaders, 64 KiB chunks paced by drain/setImmediate, 15 s deadline and 64 KiB response bound. Do not substitute chunked framing or direct handler for the original gate. The direct handler with forbidden bucket access is a separate diagnostic.

New PHASE7_DECLARED_HTTP_OBSERVATION records only bounded/sanitized outcome (complete-json, non-json-response, response-aborted/incomplete/bound, deadline or allowlisted transport category), status, allowlisted content type, presence of coordinator X-POC-Wall-Ms marker, offered bytes (not confirmation of delivery), received response bytes and known media-too-large code. Raw response, secrets and hashes are not logged. A marker present supports coordinator response attribution; an absent marker does not prove where the request stopped. No instrumentation of deployed runtime or proxy internals yet. Root cause remains OPEN pending actual diagnostics. No speculative source fix.

After observation, before asserting the required HTTP outcome, verify read-only missing/usage equals pre-request inventory, oversized object absent, committed state/revision/write identity and both preexisting media unchanged, encrypted original preserved. Poll only read-only busy responses boundedly (no retry of rejected write, no restart to clear admission). Verify unauthorized valid upload 401, wrong-owner 409, object still absent, then same-owner valid MP4 PUT 201 with exact SHA readback and unchanged state. These are authored assertions, CI REQUIRED. Strict gate requires completed response, application/json, status 413, media-too-large and coordinator marker. A non-JSON/reset/timeout remains FAIL even if all integrity subchecks pass. No continue-on-error. Dedicated job produces phase7-integrated-safety-process-profile even on test failure. Expected nine jobs, eight artifact ZIPs (if all emitted), 11240 executions if all tests reached. At last inspection 2/9 jobs concluded success (poc and storage, conclusions only); seven in progress including diagnostic job 140846395818. No fresh profile/artifact or job log verified from this candidate yet; Phase 6 reviewed evidence retained, not substituted.

### P7-C feasibility / P7-03–05

Reopened actual frozen frontend/src/lib/media-sync.js, media-store.js, media-owed.js, frontend/src/store/useStore.js, frontend/src/views/Settings.jsx and media-client.integration.test.jsx. Confirmed mechanism: media-sync obtains /api/media/missing and marks present hashes synced. For missing previously acknowledged bytes over current cap, refuse calls markRejected; HTTP permanent refusal also calls markRejected. media-store markRejected only patches rejected=true (early return if already rejected), never restores pending. pendingRefCount and unsyncedChanges depend on pending; localMediaGc under cache pressure filters !pending and can remove live referenced media; earlier unreferenced cleanup and retainOnly also require review. Previously verified PHASE5_LEGACY_ACK_PENDING_GAP and PHASE5_LEGACY_ACK_CACHE_EVICTION RISK_REPRODUCED remain; explicit restage proof is not automatic official-client remediation. Actual Settings reset immediately resets/toasts and asynchronously clearMediaAfterReset pushes, optionally sweeps (swallows sweep failure), then retainOnly(stashedMediaHashes) without confirmed commit/backup/owner fence; D1 truthful retention and safe local preservation remain unsatisfied.

A server-only HTTP fix cannot ensure the installed app re-marks its local store pending or prevents its GC/reset. Server must report actual missing hashes and reject incompatible bytes, never lie that absent bytes exist, raise caps without qualification, or manufacture acknowledgements. Source-level remediation candidate would need durable missing->owed protection before rejection/defer/backoff, preserve referenced/local-only/active/stashed sole copies, and fail closed under persistence/concurrent cleanup/identity change; guard reset on verified state/backup with truthful retention disclosure. Add focused real-store/backend failure and cold-restart regression tests before considering changes; do not treat a markRejected-only patch as sufficient. Official installed app version and native filesystem/GC behavior unverified. A source patch on migration branch does not update an installed APK/IPA. No client changes/rebuild/distribution here. If mandatory safety cannot be achieved on installed release, stop for explicit owner scope/delivery decision (compatible upstream release, separately approved client change, or alternative safe deployment; never waive safety). Physical M4 remains mandatory and staging separately unauthorized. Existing-account cutover prohibited.

#### Architecture decision gate — no speculative reread fix (awaiting owner approval)

Root cause: the current per-medium stale-read fences in useStore.js:1264–1300 are followed by ordinary unconditional writeStashes/writeJsonFile, including a second unconditional whole-inventory write for compaction. No number of pre-read comparisons can guarantee safe check-and-write against an uncoordinated writer; atomic rename alone prevents torn writes, not lost updates. Current promise queue/Web Locks at useStore.js:1164–1174 only serialize cooperating callers; no shared native process lock, legacy sync publication separately qualified. Source review at entry HEAD: frontend/package-lock.json pins @capacitor/filesystem 8.1.5 and @capacitor/core/ios/android 8.5.3 (package.json ranges not installed versions). frontend/src/lib/mobile.js readJsonFile uses Filesystem.readFile/readdir; writeJsonFile passes only path/directory/data/encoding to writeFile and catches failures; exact readback cannot detect a publication already overwritten. Android capacitor.build.gradle links capacitor-filesystem; MainActivity.java and iOS MainViewController.swift registered custom plugins do not expose a stash CAS contract. Android BackupFolderPlugin.java exposes pick/check/write/prune/release for backup folders, not an owner-bound stash transaction. Read-only upstream source comparison: ionic-team/capacitor-filesystem tag v8.1.5 tree 757d7cd309f5dbee872198efc875554ed9681f4b, src/definitions.ts WriteFileOptions path/data/directory/encoding/recursive; FilesystemPlugin interface read/write/append/delete/mkdir/readdir/stat/rename/copy and other ordinary operations, no expected version/hash, conditional replace, transaction or shared lock API. Android FilesystemPlugin.kt writeFile delegates controller.saveFile(uri,input.options); iOS FilesystemPlugin.swift writeFile dispatches .write(url,encodingMapper,recursive). No conditional expected-version input on these bridges. This is pinned source/API review, not inspection of installed native binaries, their actual filesystem concurrency or all underlying OS capabilities. No rebuild, external resource operation or dependency change.

Options:
A. Additional reread or atomic rename alone: rejected as a complete fix; confirmed final-write interleavings remain possible.
B. Cooperating native lock or transactional/CAS adapter: viable only if every writer including legacy clients honors the same authority, or the OS/storage primitive enforces conditional replace. Current pinned public adapter provides neither contract. Custom native integration or dependency replacement and official release/rebuild require separate owner approval and physical compatibility acceptance; do not simulate support.
C. Non-destructive retention / versioned acknowledgement receipts (recommended for a source-only feasibility increment, not approved or implemented): remove automatic destructive whole-file cleanup of the shared native stash from adoption; record owner/server/exact immutable entry identity plus verified acknowledgement separately, never use only key or timestamp to suppress changed/equal-timestamp divergent data. Receipt and state identity must be bound to the actually acknowledged payload and local active/media durability, with lost ACK, stale revision, owner change, restore/rewind, crash and unreadable/missing/full storage fail-closed behavior. Newer/foreign entries and raw original bytes remain recoverable; distinguish logical acknowledged state from raw retained inventory and keep stashedMediaHashes/GC/reset protection over retained originals; no silent resurrection, no cross-owner suppression, no hidden deletion. Use independent unique/versioned records only after proving no-clobber semantics under supported adapter; normal publication paths also still overwrite whole inventory and require separate review. No automatic purge; explicit export/recovery and retention capacity/privacy/account-delete policies remain gates. Old installed clients may ignore new receipts or use legacy cleanup and cannot be certified by patched JS. Source-only changes do not upgrade installed official apps. Existing mobile tests at lines 729–746 and 908 require tombstone physical markers/keptState null; prior late-window tests require actual tombstone write counts. Simply disabling cleanup changes behavior, and making write-boundary assertions green by not executing the write is invalid.
